Impact on Annual Pension Plan UAL Payments ($mil)1
• Replacing the County’s upward curving pension UAL amortizations with level debt bonds could reduce the cost and life of the County’s pension 
liability, and we expect the level debt amortization in particular would be well-received by rating agencies
• Analysis includes a $2,111,420 Contingency Reserve Fund funded by cash contributions from the County
• This scenario could produce $9,063,716 of total expected NPV savings

Pledged Revenues
(a) These amounts are from audited financial statements of the County for the years indicated. This table has not, however, been the subject of any separate audit procedures.
(b) These amounts are unaudited and subject to change upon audit and should be considered with an abundance of caution.
(c) These amounts are budgeted, constitute “forward looking” statements and should be considered with an abundance of caution.
(d) Does not include amounts allocated from the Education tax described under “State Shared Revenues” above.
(e) Pursuant to Section 11-292, Arizona Revised Statutes, the Treasurer of the State withholds an amount of the State Shared Revenues sufficient to meet the County’s portion of
the non-federal costs of providing the long-term care system in the State (“ALTCS”) from moneys otherwise payable to the County in accordance with the State’s distribution
of the State Shared Revenues. The County’s contribution is based on a fixed State formula. In the event that the State Shared Revenues withheld from the County by the
Treasurer of the State are insufficient to meet the funding requirement of ALTCS, the Treasurer of the State may withhold any other moneys payable to the County from any
available State funding source.
(f) Pursuant to Section 11-292, Arizona Revised Statutes, the Treasurer of the State withholds an amount of the State Shared Revenues determined by statutory formula for the
administrative costs of implementing certain provisions of the Arizona Health Care Cost Containment System (“AHCCCS”). If the County does not make its contribution to the
AHCCCS fund, which is used to offset certain indigent and related health care costs, the Treasurer of the State may withhold any amounts owed, plus interest retroactive to
the first date the funding was due, from the County’s State Shared Revenues.
(g) The distribution of State Shared Revenues and Vehicle License Tax Revenues is subject to change by the State Legislature and accordingly, the County is unable to covenant to
maintain State Shared Revenues or Vehicle License Tax Revenues at any particular level of coverage to debt service.
(h) The distribution of State Shared Revenues and Vehicle License Tax Revenues is subject to change by the State Legislature and accordingly, the County is unable to covenant to
maintain the State Shared Revenues or Vehicle License Tax Revenues at any particular level of coverage to debt service .

Disclosure
Pension Obligation Bonds (“POBs”) are a source of financing for unfunded actuarial liabilities of pension funds and can serve a valuable function. However, the success of a POB financing is
dependent on a number of assumptions proving to be accurate, and the failure of any of these assumptions is a risk that a government issuing POBs should consider.
Among the assumptions that are important to a POB financing, and the risks associated with those assumptions providing to be inaccurate, are the following:
• Assumption: The investment yield on the POB proceeds once deposited in the pension fund will equal or exceed the yield on the POBs. Risk: If the investment yield on the POB proceeds is less
than the yield on the POBs, and the decline is not offset by positive changes in other assumptions, the issuance of the POBs may actually increase the unfunded actuarial liability.
• Assumption: Payroll increases during the term of the POBs will be as anticipated when the unfunded actuarial liability was estimated at POB issuance. Risk: If payroll increases during the term of
the POBs exceed expectations, and the increases are not offset by positive changes in other assumptions, the POB proceeds will not suffice to cover the unfunded actuarial liability.
• Assumption: Cost of living adjustments (“COLAs”) will be as anticipated when the unfunded actuarial liability was estimated at POB issuance. Risk: If COLAs exceed expectations during the term
of the POBs, and the increases are not offset by positive changes in other assumptions, the POB proceeds will not suffice to cover the unfunded actuarial liability.
• Assumption: Various assumptions used in calculating the unfunded actuarial liability -- such as mortality rates, early retirement incentives, types of payrolls covered by the pension fund -- will be
as anticipated at the time of POB issuance. Risk: If there are reductions in mortality rates, increases in early retirement incentives, expansions of the payrolls covered by the pension plan
during the term of the POBs, and these changes are not offset by positive changes to other assumptions, the POB proceeds will not suffice to cover the unfunded actuarial liability.
In addition to analyzing potential benefits that are based on achieving assumptions made in estimating the unfunded actuarial liability, we will also analyze potential budgetary benefits or losses
based on various prospective levels of the pension systems’ earnings to assist you in gauging the likelihood of success of a POB transaction. It should be noted that potential budgetary benefits vary
from year to year. Actual benefits or losses and the success of the POB financing cannot be known until the POBs have been paid in full.
Stifel, Nicolaus & Company, Incorporated (“Stifel”) has been engaged or appointed to serve as an underwriter or placement agent with respect to a particular issuance of municipal securities to
which the attached material relates and Stifel is providing all information and advice contained in the attached material in its capacity as underwriter or placement agent for that particular issuance.
As outlined in the SEC’s Municipal Advisor Rule, Stifel has not acted, and will not act, as your municipal advisor with respect to the issuance of the municipal securities that is the subject to the
engagement.
Stifel is providing information and is declaring to the proposed municipal issuer that it has done so within the regulatory framework of MSRB Rule G-23 as an underwriter (by definition also including
the role of placement agent) and not as a financial advisor, as defined therein, with respect to the referenced proposed issuance of municipal securities. The primary role of Stifel, as an underwriter,
is to purchase securities for resale to investors in an arm’s-length commercial transaction. Serving in the role of underwriter, Stifel has financial and other interests that differ from those of the
issuer. The issuer should consult with its own financial and/or municipal, legal, accounting, tax and other advisors, as applicable, to the extent it deems appropriate.
These materials have been prepared by Stifel for the client or potential client to whom such materials are directly addressed and delivered for discussion purposes only. All terms and conditions are
subject to further discussion and negotiation. Stifel does not express any view as to whether financing options presented in these materials are achievable or will be available at the time of any
contemplated transaction. These materials do not constitute an offer or solicitation to sell or purchase any securities and are not a commitment by Stifel to provide or arrange any financing for any
transaction or to purchase any security in connection therewith and may not be relied upon as an indication that such an offer will be provided in the future. Where indicated, this presentation may
contain information derived from sources other than Stifel. While we believe such information to be accurate and complete, Stifel does not guarantee the accuracy of this information. This material
is based on information currently available to Stifel or its sources and is subject to change without notice. Stifel does not provide accounting, tax or legal advice; however, you should be aware that

Board of Supervisors Regular 8. a. 1.

Title: 250' Self-Support Wireless Cell Tower-Dry Lake - District III
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Berrin Nejad
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 27-2021
approving/denying a special use permit that allows construction of a 250’ self-support, wireless
communication tower and facility on APN# 201-13-001, Township 14 North, Range 19 East,
Section 03 of the GSRM.
Background:
Owner: Rocking Chair Ranch Inc. Agent: Cellular One, Susan Gabler.
The applicant, Cellular One, is requesting a Special Use Permit for a second tower to construct
and operate a wireless communication facility at the Dry Lake site for FirstNet equipment. The
existing tower is 295 feet and has reached max capacity. The new tower will be self-supporting,
250 feet in height and will be located to the west of the existing tower.  The new tower will be
within 40' of the exisitng tower, will help with the expansion to provide service for FirstNet,  and
will remain on the property.  
Planning and Zonning Commission heard this item at their July 15, 2021 meeting.  There was no
opposition from the public. The Commission added a stipulation to the staff recommendations
stating that all the concrete and footers will be removed once the tower is abondended and
equipment is removed. The Planning and Zonning Commission recommended approval 4-0. 
Recommendation: 
Planning and Zoning Commission and staff recommend approval of this Special Use Permit
request.

STAFF REPORT  
 
TO: Planning and Zoning Commission 
 
FROM: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director 
  
HEARING DATE: July 15, 2021   
 
 PROJECT NUMBER:  SUP 21 -010 DRY LAKE II WIRELESS COMMUNICATION 
TOWER, DISTRICT III:  A request for a Special Use Permit to 
construct a 250’ self -support, wireless communication tower and 
facility. APN# 201 -13-001, T14N, R19E, S03 of the GSRM. Owner: 
Rocking Chair Ranch Inc. Agent: Susan Gabler.  
 
 
BACKGROUND and ANALYSIS: 
 
The applicant, Cellular One, has requested a Special Use Permit for a second tower to construct and 
operate a wireless communication facility at the Dry Lake site for FirstNet equipment. The existing 
tower is 295 feet and has reached max capacity.  The new tow er will be self -support, 250 feet in 
height and will be located to the west of the existing tower. 
 
SURROUNDING USES:  
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North A- General - Vacant 
East A- General - Vacant 
South A- General - Vacant 
West A- General - Vacant 
  
APPLICABLE CODES: 
 
Zoning Ordinance: Navajo County, Zoning Ordinance addresses cell towers in the following areas:  
 
1- ARTICLE 3 - (A-GENERAL) - GENERAL ZONING DISTRICT 
Section 301 - Purpose. 
The main purpose of this A -General Zone is to provide for all the unincorporated area of the

<!-- page 647 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                                     July 15, 2021        2 | P a g e  
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
County, except land within the boundary of a reservation and outside the authority of Navajo 
County not otherwise designated for some other specific zone, to be included in the "A-General 
Zone", by this Ordinance.  No subdividing shall be conducted or approved in the "A -General 
Zone", without prior re-zoning of the land so intended.  Uses permitted in the "A-General Zone", 
include farm and non-farm residential uses, farms, recreational, institutional, commercial and 
industrial uses as specifically listed in this Article.  Other uses may be permitted as Special Uses 
under Article 20.   
 
2- Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
Section 2001 -34 
 
34. Television, wireless towers, and radio transmitter towers and stations, subject to 
approval by Federal Regulatory Agencies. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
 
Comprehensive Plan 
 
4.0 Land Use Element 
4.7 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
 
Policy 6-B:   Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses including, but 
not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping. 
 
The applicant meeting the setback standards for cellular towers.  
 
Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner. 
 
Policy 9 -F: Encourage development/installation of technology improvements including, but not 
limited to, high speed Internet lines and other communications improvements in Navajo 
County. 
The applicant has indicated that they are installing this tower to serve FirstNet network.   
 
 
ANALYSIS: 
 
The proposed self-supported lattice tower is three sided and proposal meets setbacks to keep the 
tower internally located on the property as a sort of buffer and will be in silver color to blend in with 
the natural environment .  The Navajo County Comprehensive Plan states that, to accommodate 
growth properly, development that improves communications in Navajo County should be 
encouraged.  The proposal meets the Comprehensive Plan criteria. 
 
If the conditions outlined in this report, as prepared by the Navajo County Planning and Zoning and 
Engineering Departments are applied to this project, Staff feels this project will be suitable for 
approval for a Special Use Permit.

FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised 
Statutes and Article 29.  
Notice of this item was published in the June 29th, 2021 White Mountain Independent.   Physical 
postings were made on the property on June 30, 2021 and postcards were mailed to all neighbors 
within 300 feet. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 This proposal meets policies and goals as laid out by Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which 
the property is located. 
This property is zoned A-General and as such, cellular towers are permissible uses in this zoning 
district. 
 
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
There are already existing cellular towers in the in vicinity and this tower would be compatible 
with surrounding uses.  
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 The proposed use should have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in its vicinity.

<!-- page 649 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                                     July 15, 2021        4 | P a g e  
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
1. The Special Use Permit shall permit 1 (one) wireless communication tower, not to exceed 250 
feet in height, and accessory structures necessary for the operation of the tower on the subject 
property. 
 
2. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur only in the location shown on the approved 
site plan. 
 
3. The Special Use Permit shall run with the land. 
 
4. The applicant shall obtain a Building Permit from Navajo County for such tower prior to 
commencing construction. 
 
5. The applicant must meet all State and Fede ral requirements concerning communication towers 
prior to any building permits being issued. 
 
6. The tower shall be constructed as a self- supported, three -sided lattice , as indicated in the 
supplied plans.  The owner of the tower shall maintain the tower, antennae as necessary. 
 
7. A seven (7) foot fence of material acceptable to the Planning and Zoning Director shall be 
constructed to enclose the base of the tower and necessary accessory structures.  
 
8. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period from the date of 
approval by the Board of Supervisors, this permit shall become null and void. 
 
9. Should the operator decide to abandon the use of the tower, they shall notify Navajo County 
Planning and Zoning that the tower is no longer being used. 
 
10. If the tower is no longer being utilized, the property owner shall cause, at their expense, the 
removal of all components of this tower within a 90-day period. 
 
11. The owner shall provide a 250-foot, non-buildable easement from the tower base. 
 
12. The owner shall provide reasonable cooperation to other public and private users who may be 
interested in collocation on this tower and shall consider such collocation upon commercially 
reasonable terms, unless such collocation is not technically feasible.

ITEM #5 SUP 21-010 DRY LAKE II WIRELESS COMMUNICATION TOWER, DISTRICT 
III:  A request for  a Special Use Permit to construct a 250’ self -support, wireless 
communication tower and facility. APN# 201-13-001, T14N, R19E, S03 of the GSRM. 
Owner: Rocking Chair Ranch Inc. Agent: Susan Gabler.

<!-- page 700 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
Community Development Director, Berrin Nejad, presented the item to the Commission. 
The applicant is requesting approval to construct a second cell tower at the site. The new 
tower will be 250 feet in height and will be about 50 feet to the west of the existing tower. 
She presented some photos of the area to the Commission, noting that it was A-General 
zoning and that Staff had not received comment from the public in support or against the 
project. Therefore, Staff recommended approval of the construction of a second tower , 
subject to conditions.  
 
Mr. Dennis Baker, from Cellular One, approached the Commission and said that they had 
purchased the existing tower  from Cable One, and they  have been using it  since its 
purchase. He stated that the current tower was no t built for , nor conducive to , 
communications use and  Cellular One  wants to establish a stronger network for 
emergency services, for First Net.  Microwave will be used, and they will offset with the 
existing tower.  
 
Vice-Chairman Smith thanked the applicant and asked if there was anyone in the public 
that wished to discuss the project, none were present that were either in favor of or against 
the project.  
 
Vice-Chairman Smith then asked if the Commission had questions for the applicant or 
staff.  
 
Commissioner DeCross asked if the existing tower would remain standing . Mr. Baker 
confirmed that it would.  
 
Commissioner DeCross further asked if the existing tower was for communications or 
not. Mr. Baker stated that the current tower  was made more for microwave technology, 
and that it cannot support the advanced technology that weighs more and is much bulkier 
than in the past, despite two upgrades to try and reinforce it . The existing tower is at 
capacity and constructing a new tower will be the only way to move forward and expand, 
especially with Cellular One’s interest in providing service for First Net.  
 
Commissioner DeCross further asked for more information on how close that new tower 
would be to the existing tower. Mr. Baker said the tower would be within 30 to 40 feet, and 
its placement would depend on engineering and soil stability.  
 
Commissioner DeCross asked if Cellular One was the only user on the  existing tower. 
Mr. Baker stated they had three other entities using the current tower, including Navajo 
County, AT&T, and another government entity.  
 
Commissioner DeCross asked if the existing users would be moved to the new tower . 
Mr. Baker said that everything would remain as it was, but if there were to be any upgrades 
they would have to be on the new tower.  
 
Commissioner DeCross then asked if there was anything within 300 feet of the towers. 
Mr. Baker replied that there was not anything exactly within 300 feet outside o f the 
guywires for NPC’s radio tower and noted several other towers in the surrounding area. 
 
Commissioner DeCross asked Staff about one of the pictures in the staff report, asking 
if what was pictured was a residence. Mr. Baker  explained that it was the bo ar farm and

<!-- page 701 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
said it was about half a mile away.  
 
Commissioner Murph asked about the condition on the removal of the tower at the end 
of the lifespan of the towers. Mr. Baker stated that they have in their leasing contracts an 
agreement to return the land back to the natural state. However, some landowners wished 
to keep certain things or have things done a certain way. Usually, they just sold the tower 
to someone else when a company would no longer use them, which is how they had come 
to own the existing tower. Mr. Baker further described how they were willing to remove the 
concrete but stated that he had never personally removed a tower.  
 
Commissioner Murph asked Staff if the issue had come up that the concrete would be 
removed completely. He further asked if they would amend the stipulation to add that all 
concrete be completely removed. 
 
With no further discussion, Vice-Chairman Smith called for a motion.  Commissioner 
Murph made a motion to recommend approval to the Board of Supervisors with the 
condition that all concrete and footers be removed once the tower is abandoned and the 
equipment is removed.  Commissioner Heisler seconded the motion.  Motion carried, 4 
- 0. 
 
Vice-Chairman Smith noted that the minutes from June 17, 2021 had not been voted on 
properly following the motion by Commissioner Murph and second by Commissioner 
Heisler.  Vice-Chairman Smith had the Commission return to this item for a vote. 
 
ITEM #6 SUP  21-011 SHUMWAY C1 WIRELESS COMMUNICATION TOWER, 
DISTRICT III: A request for a Special Use Permit to construct a 100’ monopole, wireless 
communication tower and facility. APN# 404-04-003U, T12N, R22E, S19 of the GSRM. 
Owner: David Jordan. Agent: Susan Gabler. 
 
Director Nejad presented the item to the Commission, showing the site for this project and 
explained that they were replacing an existing tower at this site. The tower will be used for 
the First Net equipment and the existing tower is too small for the required equipment and 
will be replaced with a 100’ monopole. Staff had received one call from the public that was 
curious about the project, though they just  asked for information . S taff recommend ed 
approval of the project, subject to conditions. 
 
Mr. Baker approached the Commission again and said that the site was very similar to the 
previous item , except that they do not have any cellular equipment there and they 
purchased the existing tower for the internet use.  Cellular One have been approached by 
emergency services to use the tower for First Net use, and they are going to be returning 
the existing site  to the landowner and will be moving the new site to the center of the 
property. The monopole will have a much smaller footprint  than other types of towers .  
Cellular One will have more ability to provide a variety of services and equipment, and the 
monopole will be pre-built to handle the needs for the loads of such services.  
 
Commissioner Murph again noted the condition for removal of the concrete. The owner 
of the property, David Jordan, came forward and expressed interest in keeping the existing 
tower for  his own personal  purposes, following the cessation of the allowed use , and 
keeping the concrete if he desired. Following  discussion, Staff recommended that the 
property owner apply for a Special Use Permit once the tower was no longer in use if they 
wished to keep it for other uses.

<!-- page 702 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
Commissioner Murph asked Staff to look into what to do if the property owners wish to 
keep equipment in the future. 
 
With there being no further discussion, Vice-Chairman Smith called for  a motion.  
Commissioner Murph made a motion to recommend Approval, with the condition that all 
concrete and footers be removed once the tower is abandoned and the equipment is 
removed.  Commissioner Heisler seconded the motion.  Motion carried, 4 - 0. 
 
 
ITEM #7– REPORT FROM STAFF TO THE COMMISSION: 
 
Commissioners may use this time to offer additional comments regarding any item on this 
agenda or any other topic; and the Commission may direct Development Services 
Department staff to study or provide additional information on topics of the Commissions’ 
choosing.   
 
With there being no further business to come before the Planning and Zoning 
Commission, a motion was made to adjourn the meeting by Commissioner Heisler. 
Commissioner Murph seconded the motion.  The meeting was adjourned at 6:41 p.m.

RESOLUTION ____-21 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS APPROVING A 
SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR A 250 FEET WIRELESS COMMUNICATION TOWER-DRY 
LAKE 
 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on February 26, 2021 
by Susan Gabler to allow a 250 feet self-support wireless communication tower; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the foll owing real p roperty in Navajo County: 
APN 201-13-001, which is a portion of Township 14 North, Range 19 East, Section 03; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on July 15, 2021; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Superv isors after c onsidering the te stimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that the 
Special Use Permit is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare a nd should be 
approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supe rvisors hereby 
approves the Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. The Special Use Permit shall permit 1 (one) wireless communication tower, not to 
exceed 250 feet in height, and accessory structures necessary for the operation of the 
tower on the subject property. 
 
2. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur only  in the location shown on the 
approved site plan. 
 
3. The Special Use Permit shall run with the land. 
 
4. The applicant shall obtain a Building Permit from Navajo County for such tower prior to 
commencing construction. 
 
5. The applicant must meet all State and Fede ral requirements concerning communication 
towers prior to any building permits being issued.

<!-- page 704 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
6. The tower shall be constructed as a self- supported, three-sided lattice, as  indicated in 
the supplied plans.  The owner of the tower shall maintain the tower, an tennae as 
necessary. 
 
7. A seven (7) foot fence of material acceptable to the Planning and Zoning Director shall 
be constructed to enclose the base of the tower and necessary accessory structures.  
 
8. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period from the 
date of approval by the Board of Supervisors, this permit shall become null and void. 
 
9. Should the operator decide to abandon the use of the to wer, they shall notify Navajo 
County Planning and Zoning that the tower is no longer being used. 
 
10. If the tower is no longer being utilized, the property owner shall cause, at their expense, 
the removal of all components of this tower within a 90-day period. 
 
11. The owner shall provide a 250-foot, non-buildable easement from the tower base. 
 
12. The owner shall provide reasonable cooperation to other public and private users who 
may be interested in collocation on this tower and shall consider such collocation upon  
commercially reasonable terms unless such collocation is not technically feasible. 
 
13. All the concrete and footers will be removed once the tower is abandoned, and 
equipment is removed.  
 
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ 
yeas and ______ nays on this 14th day of September 2021.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________

RESOLUTION ____-21 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING A 
SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR A 250 FEET WIRELESS COMMUNICATION TOWER-DRY 
LAKE 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit  was duly filed on  February 26, 2021 
by Susan Gabler to allow a 250 feet self-support wireless communication tower; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the foll owing real p roperty in Navajo County: APN 
201-13-001, which is a portion of Township 14 North, Range 19 East, Section 03; and 
 
WHEREAS, the applica tion was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on July 15, 2021; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervis ors after considering the testimony and 
other evidence presented at the hearing, as we ll a s the recommendat ions of staff, found that the 
Special Use Permit is not consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should be 
denied; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby deny the 
Special Use Permit.

Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 28-2021
approving/denying a special use permit that allows construction of a 100 foot monopole, wireless
communication tower and facility on APN# 404-04-003U, Township 12 North, Range 22 East,
Section 19 of the GSRM.
Background:
Owner: David & Judith Jordan, Agent: Cellular One,  Susan Gabler.
The applicant, Cellular One, has requested a Special Use Permit to replace the existing tower to
construct and operate a wireless communication facility at the Shumway site for FirstNet
equipment. The existing tower is too small and will be replaced with 100’ monopole tower.  The
property is in RU-1, Rural Zoning District. Staff had received one call from the public that was
curious about the project, though they just asked for information. 
Planning and Zoning Commission heard this item on their July 15, 2021 meeting.  There was no
opposition from the public. The Commission added a stipulation to the staff recommendations
stating that all the concrete and footers will be removed once the tower is abondended and
equipment is removed. The Planning and Zonning Commission recommended approval 4-0. 
Recommendation: 
Planning and Zoning Commission and staff recommend approval of this Special Use Permit
request.

STAFF REPORT  
 
TO: Planning and Zoning Commission 
 
FROM: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director 
  
HEARING DATE: July 15, 2021   
 
 PROJECT NUMBER:  SUP 21 -011 SHUMWAY C1 WIRELESS COMMUNICATION 
TOWER, DISTRICT III: A request for a Special Use Permit to 
construct a 100’ monopole, wireless communication tower and facility. 
APN# 404-04-003U, T12N, R22E, S19 of the GSRM. Owner: David 
Jordan. Agent: Susan Gabler. 
 
 
BACKGROUND and ANALYSIS: 
 
The applicant, Cellular One, has requested a Special Use Permit to replace the existing tower to 
construct and operate a wireless communication facility at the Shumway site for FirstNet equipment. 
The existing tower is too small and will be replaced with 100’ monopole tower.  The property is in 
RU-1, Rural Zoning District.  
 
 
SURROUNDING USES:  
 
Direction Zoning/Use 
North RU-1, Rural Zoning District, One acre minimum  
East RU-1, Rural Zoning District, One acre minimum  
South RU-1, Rural Zoning District, One acre minimum 
West RU-1, Rural Zoning District, One acre minimum

<!-- page 720 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                                     July 15, 2021        2 | P a g e  
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
APPLICABLE CODES: 
 
Zoning Ordinance: Navajo County, Zoning Ordinance addresses cell towers in the following areas:  
 
1- ARTICLE 8 - (RURAL 1) – RURAL ZONING DISTRICT,  
 ONE (1) ACRE PER DWELLING UNIT 
Section 801 - Purpose. 
The principal purpose of this district is to conserve and protect open land uses, as well as foster 
orderly growth in rural areas, and prevent urban and agricultural land use conflicts.  The 
primary purpose of requiring large minimum parcels of not less than one (1) acre in area i s to 
discourage small lot or residential subdivisions where public facilities such as water, sewage 
disposal, parks and playgrounds, and governmental services such as police and fire protection 
are not available or could not reasonably be made currently available.  principal uses permitted 
in this zoning district include both farm and non -farm residential uses, farms, and recreational 
and institutional uses. 
 
2- Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
Section 2001 -34 
 
34. Television, wireless towers, and radio transmitter towers and stations, subject to 
approval by Federal Regulatory Agencies. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
 
Comprehensive Plan 
 
4.0 Land Use Element 
4.7 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
 
Policy 6-B:   Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses including, but 
not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping. 
 
The applicant is meeting the setback standards for cellular towers.  
 
Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner. 
 
Policy 9 -F: Encourage development/installation of technology improvements including, but not 
limited to, high speed Internet lines and other communications improvements in Navajo 
County. 
The applicant has indicated that they are installing this tower to serve FirstNet network.   
 
 
ANALYSIS: 
The proposed 100-feet monopole tower is a replacement of the existing tower to meet the needs of 
FirstNet that is dedicated Broadband Network for Emergency First Responders.   
 
The Navajo County Comprehensive Plan states that, to accommodate growth properly, development

<!-- page 721 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                                     July 15, 2021        3 | P a g e  
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
that improves communications in Navajo County should be encouraged .  The proposal meets the 
Comprehensive Plan criteria. 
 
If the conditions outlined in this report, as prepared by the Navajo County Planning and Zoning and 
Engineering Departments are applied to this project, Staff feels this project will be suitable for 
approval for a Special Use Permit. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised 
Statutes and Article 29.  
Notice of this item was published in the June 29th, 2021 White Mountain Independent.   Physical 
postings were made on the property on June 30, 2021 and postcards were mailed to all neighbors 
within 300 feet. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 This proposal meets policies and goals as laid out by Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which 
the property is located. 
This property is zoned RU-1 and as such, cellular towers are  permissible uses in this zoning 
district. 
 
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
There was already existing cellular tower on the property, this will be a replacement, therefore 
it will be compatible with surrounding uses.  
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 The proposed use should have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in its vicinity.

following conditions: 
 
1. The Special Use Permit shall permit 1 (one) monopole wireless communication tower, not to 
exceed 100 feet in height, and accessory structures necessary for the operation of the tower on 
the subject property. 
 
2. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur only in the location shown on the approved 
site plan. 
 
3. The Special Use Permit shall run with the land.

commencing construction. 
 
5. The applicant must meet all State and Federal requirements concerning communication towers 
prior to any building permits being issued. 
 
6. The tower shall be constructed as a monopole, as indicated in the supplied plans.  The owner of 
the tower shall maintain the tower, antennae as necessary. 
 
7. A seven (7) foot fence of material acceptable to the Planning and Zoning Director shall be 
constructed to enclose the base of the tower and necessary accessory structures.  
 
8. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period from the date of 
approval by the Board of Supervisors, this permit shall become null and void. 
 
9. Should the operator decide to abandon the use of the tower, they shall notify Navajo County 
Planning and Zoning that the tower is no longer being used. 
 
10. If the tower is no longer being utilized, the property owner shall cause, at their expense, the 
removal of all components of this tower within a 90-day period. 
 
11. The owner shall provide a 100-foot, non-buildable easement from the tower base.

interested in collocation on this tower and shall consider such collocation upon commercially

condition that all concrete and footers be removed once the tower is abandoned and the 
equipment is removed.  Commissioner Heisler seconded the motion.  Motion carried, 4 
- 0. 
 
Vice-Chairman Smith noted that the minutes from June 17, 2021 had not been voted on 
properly following the motion by Commissioner Murph and second by Commissioner 
Heisler.  Vice-Chairman Smith had the Commission return to this item for a vote. 
 
ITEM #6 SUP  21-011 SHUMWAY C1 WIRELESS COMMUNICATION TOWER, 
DISTRICT III: A request for a Special Use Permit to construct a 100’ monopole, wireless 
communication tower and facility. APN# 404-04-003U, T12N, R22E, S19 of the GSRM. 
Owner: David Jordan. Agent: Susan Gabler. 
 
Director Nejad presented the item to the Commission, showing the site for this project and 
explained that they were replacing an existing tower at this site. The tower will be used for 
the First Net equipment and the existing tower is too small for the required equipment and 
will be replaced with a 100’ monopole. Staff had received one call from the public that was 
curious about the project, though they just  asked for information . S taff recommend ed 
approval of the project, subject to conditions. 
 
Mr. Baker approached the Commission again and said that the site was very similar to the 
previous item , except that they do not have any cellular equipment there and they 
purchased the existing tower for the internet use.  Cellular One have been approached by 
emergency services to use the tower for First Net use, and they are going to be returning 
the existing site  to the landowner and will be moving the new site to the center of the 
property. The monopole will have a much smaller footprint  than other types of towers .  
Cellular One will have more ability to provide a variety of services and equipment, and the 
monopole will be pre-built to handle the needs for the loads of such services.  
 
Commissioner Murph again noted the condition for removal of the concrete. The owner 
of the property, David Jordan, came forward and expressed interest in keeping the existing 
tower for  his own personal  purposes, following the cessation of the allowed use , and 
keeping the concrete if he desired. Following  discussion, Staff recommended that the 
property owner apply for a Special Use Permit once the tower was no longer in use if they 
wished to keep it for other uses.

<!-- page 770 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
Commissioner Murph asked Staff to look into what to do if the property owners wish to 
keep equipment in the future. 
 
With there being no further discussion, Vice-Chairman Smith called for  a motion.  
Commissioner Murph made a motion to recommend Approval, with the condition that all 
concrete and footers be removed once the tower is abandoned and the equipment is 
removed.  Commissioner Heisler seconded the motion.  Motion carried, 4 - 0. 
 
 
ITEM #7– REPORT FROM STAFF TO THE COMMISSION: 
 
Commissioners may use this time to offer additional comments regarding any item on this 
agenda or any other topic; and the Commission may direct Development Services 
Department staff to study or provide additional information on topics of the Commissions’ 
choosing.   
 
With there being no further business to come before the Planning and Zoning 
Commission, a motion was made to adjourn the meeting by Commissioner Heisler. 
Commissioner Murph seconded the motion.  The meeting was adjourned at 6:41 p.m.

RESOLUTION ____-21 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS APPROVING A 
SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR A 100 FEETMONOPOLE WIRELESS COMMUNICATION 
TOWER-SHUMWAY 
 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on February 26, 2021 
by Susan Gabler to allow a 100 feet monopole self-support wireless communication tower; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the foll owing real p roperty in Navajo County: 
APN 404-04-003U, which is a portion of Township 12 North, Range 22 East, Section 19; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on July 15, 2021; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Superv isors after c onsidering the te stimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that the 
Special Use Permit is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare a nd should be 
approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supe rvisors hereby 
approves the Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. The Special Use Permit shall permit 1 (one) monopole wireless communication tower, 
not to exceed 100 feet in height, and accessory structures necessary for the operation of 
the tower on the subject property. 
 
2. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to o ccur only in the location shown on the 
approved site plan. 
 
3. The Special Use Permit shall run with the land. 
 
4. The applicant shall obtain a Building Permit from Navajo County for such tower prior to 
commencing construction. 
 
5. The applicant must meet all State and Federal requirements concerning communication 
towers prior to any building permits being issued.

<!-- page 772 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
6. The tower shall be constructed as a monopole, as indicated in the supplied plans.  The 
owner of the tower shall maintain the tower, antennae as necessary. 
 
7. A seven (7) foot fence of material acceptable to the Planning and Zoning Director shall 
be constructed to enclose the base of the tower and necessary accessory structures.  
 
8. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period from the 
date of approval by the Board of Supervisors, this permit shall become null and void. 
 
9. Should the operator decide to abandon the use of the tow er, they shall notify Navajo 
County Planning and Zoning that the tower is no longer being used. 
 
10. If the tower is no longer being utilized, the property owner shall cause, at their expense, 
the removal of all components of this tower within a 90-day period. 
 
11. The owner shall provide a 100-foot, non-buildable easement from the tower base. 
 
12. The owner shall provide reasonable cooperation to other public and private users who 
may be interested in collocation on this tower and shall consider such collocation upon 
commercially reasonable terms, unless such collocation is not technically feasible. 
 
13. All the concrete and footers will be removed once the tower is abandoned and 
equipment is removed.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8. b. 1. a.        
Meeting Date: 09/14/2021  
Title: B21-07-022 Victory Heights Road Maintenance District Contract - District IV
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Eileen Smith, Account Specialist
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Award of Contract #B21-07-022 Victory Heights Road Maintenance District to
Cameron Crandell.
Background:
On August 21, 2006 Victory Heights Road Maintenance District was formed by Resolution
#68-06 and on March 13, 2007 the Board of Directors gave approval to go out to bid for the road
maintenance of the District.  Since the existing contract expired in July 2021, the Victory Heights
Advisory Board decided to continue contracting out their road maintenance work and coordinated
with the County to procure those maintenance services.  Like the previous one, the term of this
contract is one year, with the potential of four (4) one-year renewals.
 
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 2:00 p.m. M.S.T., September 08, 2021, in strict accordance with
the specifications on file in the office of the Navajo County Department of Public Works. No bids
were accepted after 2:00 p.m. M.S.T.  The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 2:15 p.m.
 The Bid Notice was sent to local contractors and only one bid was received.  Cameron
Crandell has an active contractor's license.
 
Recommendation: 
Staff and the Victory Heights  Advisory Committee has reviewed the one bid received and is
recommending the award of the contract to Cameron Crandell.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2022
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted in the Victory Heights Road Maintenance District.

CONTRACT FOR ROAD MAINTENANCE 
 
THIS CONTRACT FOR ROAD MAINTENANCE (“Contract’) is made and entered into as of 
September 14, 2021 (the “Effective Date”), by and between Victory Heights Road Maintenance District (the 
“District”) and Cameron Crandell (the “Contractor”). 
 
This Contract sets forth the parties’ understanding in regard to road maintenance, snow removal 
and related a ctivities to be performed on approximately 2.95 of unpaved public roads within the District 
pursuant to Invitation for Bid # B21 -07-022.  The Invitation for Bids and the related Description of the Work, 
Technical Specifications and Map of the District, as well as the Contractor’s Proposal, Bid Schedule and 
Statement of Equipment, are hereby incorporated by reference and shall constitute a part of this Contract 
as though fully set forth herein.  The Map of the District identifies the roads to be maintained p ursuant to 
this Contract. 
 
1. THE WORK 
 
 The Contractor shall perform road maintenance in accordance with the Description of the Work and 
the Technical Specifications at the unit prices set forth in the Bid Schedule.  The Contractor shall develop 
a mainten ance schedule in consultation with the District’s Advisory Committee and Superintendent of 
Streets and shall also perform maintenance on an as -needed basis as the Advisory Committee or its 
representative may request.  
 
 The Contractor shall perform snow rem oval in accordance with the Description of the Work and the 
Technical Specifications at the unit prices set forth in the Bid Schedule.  Snow removal shall be performed 
as the Advisory Committee or its representative may request.  The Contractor shall respo nd promptly to 
such requests so as to minimize the inconvenience to the residents of the District.  
 
 For other work identified in the Bid Schedule – i.e., cindering, culvert installation, replacement and 
repair, and regarding and fill – the Contractor shal l respond within five days to the Advisory Committee’s 
requests for such work and shall complete each project in a timely manner.  
 
 The Contractor warrants that all work shall be performed in a professional and workmanlike manner 
and shall be subject to th e approval of the Advisory Committee as well as the District’s Superintendent of 
Streets and District Engineer.  Any deficiencies or defects in the work identified by the Superintendent of 
Streets or District Engineer shall be promptly corrected at no addi tional charge to the District.  
 
2. PAYMENT 
 
 The Contractor shall bill the District by the tenth day of each month for all work performed during 
the preceding month.  The Contractor’s invoices shall be in sufficient detail to allow the Advisory Committee 
and the Board of Directors to identify all labor performed and materials provided.  The District shall make 
payment within 30 days after receipt of each invoice.  If any amount is disputed by the District, the 
undisputed balance shall be paid during the 30 -day period. 
 
3. TERM / TERMINATION 
 
The initial term of this Contract shall be one year from the Effective Date.  Thereafter, this Contract 
may be renewed for up to four additional one -year terms with the written consent of both parties.  The 
parties shall meet no later than 30 days before the expiration of each one -year term to attempt to negotiate 
a renewal. In no event shall the prices set forth on the Bid Schedule be increased by more than 5% from 
one year to the next, and any request for a n increase shall be supported by evidence of the Contractor’s 
increased costs for labor, equipment, materials or supplies.

<!-- page 786 -->
Either party may terminate this Contract upon 120 days written notice to the other.  In addition, the 
District may terminate this Co ntract upon written notice if any deficiency or defect in the Contractor’s work 
or performance hereunder remains uncured 30 days after the District has given the Contractor written notice 
of such deficiency or defect.  
 
4. INDEMNIFICATION 
 
 To the fullest e xtent allowed by law, the Contractor shall indemnify, defend and hold harmless the 
District and Navajo County and their respective officials, officers and employees from and against any and 
all claims, actions, lawsuits, liabilities, judgments, expenses an d costs arising from or in any way relating 
to the Contractor’s performance under this Contract.  The provisions of this paragraph shall survive the 
termination or expiration of this Contract.  
 
5. INSURANCE 
 
 The Contractor, at its sole cost and expense, f or the full term of this Contract, shall obtain and 
maintain in force all of the following insurance coverages and requirements. All coverage shall be primary 
coverage in regard to the District, and any insurance maintained by the District shall be excess of the 
Contractor’s coverage and shall not contribute to it.  
 
 If the Contractor utilizes one or more subcontractors in the performance of this Contract, the 
Contractor shall obtain and maintain in force Independent Contractor’s Insurance as to each subcon tractor 
or otherwise provide evidence of insurance coverage for each subcontractor equivalent to that required of 
the Contractor. 
 
 A.  Types of Insurance and Minimum Limits  
 
  (1)  Worker’s Compensation in the minimum statutorily required coverage amounts . This 
insurance coverage shall not be required if the Contractor has no employees and certifies to this fact.  
 
  (2)  Automobile Liability Insurance for each of the Contractor’s vehicles used in the 
performance of this Contract, including owned, leased or  hired vehicles, in the minimum amount of 
$500,000 combined single limit per occurrence for bodily injury and property damage.  
 
  (3)  Comprehensive or Commercial General Liability Insurance coverage in the minimum 
amount of $1,000,000 combined single limi t, including coverage for: (a) bodily injury, (b) personal injury, (c) 
broad-form property damage, (d) contractual liability, and (e) cross -liability. 
 
 B.  Other Insurance Provisions 
 
  (1)  If any insurance coverage required in this Contract is provided on a “Claims Made” 
rather than “Occurrence” form, the Contractor agrees to maintain the required coverage for a period of three 
years after the expiration of this Contract (hereinafter “post Contract coverage”) and any extensions thereof.   
The Contractor may maintain the required post Contract coverage by renewal or purchase of prior acts or 
tail coverage. 
 
  (2)  All required Automobile and Comprehensive or Commercial General Liability Insurance 
shall be endorsed to contain the following clause:  
 
“Navajo County and Victory Heights Road Maintenance District and their 
respective, officials, officers, employees, agents and volunteers are added 
as additional insureds as respects the operations and activities of, or on 
behalf of, the named insured perf ormed under Contract with Victory 
Heights  
Road Maintenance District.”

<!-- page 787 -->
(3)  All required insurance policies shall be endorsed to contain the following clause:  
“This insurance shall not be canceled until after 30 days written notice has 
been given to: Of fice Manager, Navajo County Department of Public 
Works, P.O. Box 668, Holbrook, AZ 86025.”  
 
  (4)  The Contractor agrees to provide its insurance brokers with a full copy of these 
insurance provisions and to provide the District on or before the Effective Date with certificates of insurance 
for all required coverages. All certificates of insurance shall be delivered or sent to: Office Manager, Navajo 
County Department of Public Works, P.O. Box 668, Holbrook, AZ 86025.  
 
6. CERTIFICATIONS 
 
A. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-4401, Contractor and its subcontractors warrant compliance with all 
federal immigration laws and regulations relating to their employees and compliance with the 
E-verify requirements under A.R.S. § 23 -214(A).  Contractor’s or any subcontractor’s breac h 
of this warranty shall be deemed a material breach of the Contract and may result in termination 
of the Contract by County.   County may randomly inspect the books and records of Contractor 
and its subcontractors to verify compliance with this warranty.  Contractor and its 
subcontractors warrant to keep their books and records open for random inspection by County 
during normal business hours.  
 
B. Pursuant to A.R.S. §§ 35 -391.06 and 35 -393.06, Contractor certifies that it does not have a 
scrutinized business o peration in Sudan or Iran.   The term “scrutinized business operation” 
shall have the meaning set forth in A.R.S. §§ 35 -391 or 35-393, as applicable.  Any falsification 
in regard to this warranty shall be deemed a material breach of the Contract and may res ult in 
termination of the Contract by County  
 
 IN WITNESS WHEREOF,  the duly authorized representatives of the parties have executed this 
Contract as of the Effective Date.  
 
   VICTORY HEIGHTS ROAD MAINTENANCE DISTRICT
